=== PAGE 3 ===
Evergreen Funding Sequence  If you are unsure of what banks to go to or what order to go to them, this pre-built funding sequence will give you direction. Apply to these banks in the order you see below. Keep in mind that you might have to adjust what banks you go to on this list based on the availability of that bank in your client's state, as well as the number of inquiries they have on a particular credit bureau.  American Express   - They soft pull Experian conditionally. Start the sequence with AMEX only if your client already has a relationship with AMEX via a personal credit card. This will not only ensure they get a soft pull, but it also increases their chances for a higher limit on the AMEX business cards. If they did not have any prior relationship with AMEX. The first inquiry for Experian should always go to Chase since they are inquiry sensitive and give out generous limits.  0% Card Options:  ●   AMEX Business Blue Plus  ●   AMEX Business Blue Cash  Data Points:  ●   Annual Revenue: $325k - $350k  ●   Annual Income:   $115k -$125k  ●   Other Recommendations: “Estimated Monthly Spend” on app $18k-$25  Citizens   (Regional Bank) - They soft pull Experian personal and hard pull Experian business. They give out generous limits, and since they are a soft pull, you want to go to them early in the sequence if they are available in your client’s state.  0% Card Options:  ●   Citizens Bank Business Credit Card  Data Points  ●   Annual Revenue: $750k - $925k

You now have an evergreen funding sequence you can use for your clients. However, what happens if you get a client that already has funding from some of the banks on this list? What if they have negative relationships with any of them or just more funding beyond what’s on this list? In any of those situations, I would recommend you find the state that your client’s business is registered in, within the following sections of this document, to find additional banks and funding options you can use for your clients.
